本
先週（9/28～10/5）発表の主な経済指標

資に上振れ余地。利上げやコスト高への耐性を確認する結果

日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
3
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― 一方、非製造業は、長雨を受けて小売や対個人サービス、宿泊・飲食サービスが悪化
 2026年度の経常利益計画は、製造業中心に大幅上方修正。9月調査時点としては過去5年間で最高の増益計画
― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

調査時点では過去5年
間で最高の増益計画

日本：設備投資は高水準を維持。製造業は業績対比でなお上振れ余地
4
 2026年度の設備投資計画は前年度比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持
― 製造業は6月調査からほぼ横ばいの同＋7.1％。非製造業は同＋11.8％と1.1％Ptの上方修正
 AI需要による好業績を踏まえれば製造業の投資計画にはやや慎重さがにじむ内容
― 一方、生産・営業用設備判断DIは2025年度後半以降、設備不足方向への動きが鮮明。高水準の内部資金も踏まえれ
ば、増産投資などを中心に今後の設備投資が上振れる余地
― なお、今後の利上げが企業の投資姿勢に及ぼす影響には留意
25年度後半以降、急速
な設備不足超を示唆

日本：企業の価格設定姿勢はなお強い。仕入価格再上昇にも警戒
5
 大企業・製造業の販売価格判断DIは前期並みの高水準を維持。企業は積極的な価格転嫁姿勢を継続
―6・7月の油価下落等を背景に素材業種の仕入価格判断DIが低下。対して販売価格判断DIは横ばい。コスト低下局面で
も販売価格を維持しており、企業利益の押し上げ要因に
 企業の中長期的な物価見通しも2％台半ばと、日銀の物価目標を上回る水準で高止まり
― 足元の原油価格再上昇やタンカー運賃高騰を受け、仕入価格が再び上昇する可能性には留意。企業の価格設定姿勢
が一段と強まるリスクも
販売価格判断DI・仕入価格判断DI（大企業・製造業） 物価全般の見通し（全規模・全産業）

日本：利上げでも企業金融は安定。企業部門の金利耐性を確認
6
 資金繰り判断は総じて底堅い。これまでの利上げの影響は限定的
― 利上げの影響が懸念された中小企業の資金繰り判断は横ばい圏の推移。価格転嫁の進展などを受けた事業環境の改
善が資金繰りを支えている構図
― 金利上昇局面でも銀行貸出は増加基調を維持しており、企業の資金需要も底堅く推移
 企業収益や資金繰りが金利上昇への耐性を示す中、金融環境はなお緩和的
― 市場のターミナルレート予想は2.5％程度まで上昇し、日銀の中立金利推計レンジ上限に接近

（%）
欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
7
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退
石油製品の製品別消費割合の国際比較
（出所）Energy Institute より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
EUの石油製品消費はディーゼル・軽油が中心

### Page 9

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
8
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算
（出所）EIA、IEA、OPEC、各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

注目点②G7備蓄放出でもくすぶるディーゼル供給不安

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同+8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建設・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

9
（出所） 米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇
 対人接触度の高い業種では雇用に底堅さ、AIによる代替が進みにくい可能性
― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調
 失業率は4.2％と小幅悪化も引き続き低水準を維持
― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様

中国：景況感は急速に改善。建設業が持ち直し。政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ
 9月の総合PMIは50.7と、前月（49.5）から大幅に上昇。景況感が急速に改善。建設業が大きく持ち直し
― 製造業PMIが50.1（前月：49.8）、非製造業PMIが50.2（前月：49.0）とともに節目回復。製造業の生産（51.7）が堅調
― 建設業PMIは50.3と、前月（46.9）から急上昇。異常気象の影響はく落と財政予算執行の本格化があるとみられる
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え

### Page 13

 中国のASEAN向け輸出品は、ASEANの製造品と競合。競合分野は一般機械や玩具、食品など幅広に
― 一般機械等に加え、ベトナムは電子機器や紙・木・繊維等の軽工業品、インドネシアは輸送機械でも競合度が高い
 中国製品流入が強まる中、ASEANはアンチダンピング、セーフガード措置に加え、越境ECへの規制強化などで対応
― 素材・中間財についてはアンチダンピング措置で対応。最終財では、低価格輸入品への課税や価格規制で応じる方向
― 中国企業等による規制回避を懸念し、ASEAN側が対象品目拡張や反迂回措置を強化する動きもあるが、こうした措置
の実効性は不透明
アジア：中国製品との競争激化でASEANの防衛策が拡大

 2026年以降に相違が目立つ台湾と韓国におけるAI需要の恩恵

日本：コアCPIは政策補助効果のはく落を主因に加速。その背後で徐々に高まるインフレ圧力
14
 9月の都区部コアCPIは前年比＋2.7％（前月：同＋1.8％）と大幅加速。政策要因のはく落に加え、価格転嫁の影響も拡大
― 東京都で実施された水道基本料金無償化・保育所保育料無償化の影響がはく落し、全体の伸びを＋0.56％Pt押し上げ

日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？

Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？

「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦 Fed 決策與財報
2026 年 9 月 15 日
一、 9/14 美股下挫、美債殖利率曲線熊市趨平，且油價大漲
1. 股票指數：
⚫ 道瓊工業指數下跌 0.29%，收 52,421.20 點；
⚫ S&P 500 指數下挫 0.48%，收 7,619.98 點；
⚫ 那斯達克綜合指數下跌 0.56%，收 26,186.41 點；
⚫ 費城半導體指數下跌 5.86%，收 11,131.28 點。
2. 當日重要個股表現：
⚫ 在科技類股方面，輝達下跌約 3.4%、美光下跌 5.3%、邁威爾下跌 7.3%；

⚫ 通訊類股方面，Netflix 上漲 3.8%、Take-Two 上漲 3.5%、Fox 上漲 4.6%；
⚫ 軟體類股方面 ，ServiceNow、Adobe、Workday 等軟體股則上漲約 4% 至
7.4%。
3. 美國債市漲跌不一、殖利率曲線走勢熊市趨平。2 年期美國公債殖利率上升 3.24 個基
點至 4.66%，10 年期殖利率上升 2.06 個基點至 4.99%，10 年期殖利率一度升破 5%，
而 30 年期殖利率則下跌約 0.76 個基點至 5.34%。短率上升，反映市場對未來美國聯
準會升息的預期。
4. 油價因中東衝突與供給風險再度升高而上漲 ，WTI 原油上漲 1.81%，收在每桶 101.86
美元，布蘭特原油則上漲 1.56%，收在 106.24 美元。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. AI 開發放緩疑慮，壓抑硬體需求預期：Anthropic 執行長 Dario Amodei 於上週末
呼籲放慢前沿 AI 能力提升速度，並提出第三方安全評估與業界協調方案，使 AI 擴

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
張節奏成為新的不確定因素。我們研判，半導體位於 AI 建置支出的上游，股價對新
增算力投資的成長速度尤其敏感。即使既有訂單未立即改變，只要市場降低對未來數
季增速的期待，GPU、高頻寬記憶體及網通設備供應鏈的評價便可能先行調整；目前
仍須區分投資預期降溫與實際訂單下修。
2. 中東能源設施遇襲，油價推升通膨風險：沙烏地阿拉伯能源設施及中東船舶遇襲，引
發供應疑慮，布蘭特原油期貨 9 月 14 日結算價升至每桶 106.24 美元。油價持續偏
高可能透過運輸與生產成本以及民眾實質購買力影響企業獲利；若進一步傳導至通膨

預期，將延長貨幣政策維持緊縮的時間，對股市評價形成壓力。
3. 十年美債殖利率一度突破 5%，評價與融資承壓：美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中突破
5%，為 2023 年以來首見。殖利率上升提高未來獲利的折現率，對依賴長期成長預
期支撐評價的股票較不利。另一方面，資料中心若仰賴借款擴張，融資成本上升也會
提高投資報酬門檻，可能影響新增建置計畫，形成 AI 供應鏈面臨的另一項壓力。
4. 通膨月增回升 ， 升息前觀望氣氛加重 ：BLS 於 9 月 11 日公布 ，8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%
，高於 7 月的 0.1%，核心 CPI 月增率也由 0.2% 升至 0.3%。我們認為，短期物
價動能回升，加上能源價格偏高，提高聯準會維持緊縮政策的壓力。投資人除了評估
當次升息，也須重新衡量後續利率高點與維持時間，增加 FOMC 會議前高波動股票
的調整壓力。
5. 成長與評價雙重重估，放大半導體跌幅：本波股市回檔壓力同時來自獲利成長預期與
市場願給的評價倍數。AI 開發放緩疑慮影響未來營收想像，殖利率上行則壓低未來獲

利的現值。當股價已反映較高成長期待，只要新增訊息不如預期，即使當期營收仍強
勁，也可能出現明顯修正。後續應觀察獲利上修能否抵銷評價收縮，不能僅以跌幅大
就認定股價便宜。

不另行通知。
三、市場看法
1. 龍頭財報仍具成長，支持中期正向看法：輝達 8 月 26 日公布的 2027 會計年度第
二季資料中心營收達 890 億美元、年增 117%，並預估第三季總營收 1,080 億美元
、上下浮動 2%。博通 9 月 2 日公布的 2026 會計年度第三季 AI 半導體營收為
167 億美元、年增 221%，並預估第四季達 217 億美元。上述實績與公司展望，是
我們對 AI 核心供應鏈維持中期正向看法的依據；惟這些財測發布於本次事件之前，
仍須追蹤後續更新。
2. 放緩方案尚待落地，需求影響須分項評估：Anthropic 執行長 Amodei 明確表示，放

緩不代表停止模型訓練，但也提出研議訓練算力等限制，因此實際影響取決於後續執
行方式。既有模型的商業使用、推論與安全測試仍可能帶來算力需求，惟能否抵銷前
沿訓練投資的放緩，必須由使用量、訂單與收入驗證。此時適合保留成長觀點，同時
提高對支出變化的敏感度。
3. 科技股仍有輪動，軟體與半導體強弱分明：9/14 當日企業軟體與資安股逆勢上漲，顯
示科技股內部仍有輪動與受惠程度的差異。我們認為，若前沿 AI 開發放緩，部分既
有軟體業者面臨的競爭及產品替代壓力可能減輕；AI 安全疑慮升高，也可能強化資安
、權限控管及企業治理需求。相較之下，半導體更直接承受新增建置支出預期變化。
因此，9/14 當日費半重挫尚不足以推論整體科技需求同步轉弱；後續宜比較訂單能見
度、訂閱收入、獲利與現金流，單日相對強勢仍須由基本面延續性驗證。
4. 升息機率約九成，會後政策態度才是關鍵：依 Reuters 於美東 9 月 14 日收盤報導
引述的 CME FedWatch 資料，本次升息一碼的機率約 90%。若美東 9 月 16 日

FOMC 確實升息 0.25 個百分點，利率區間將升至 3.75% 至 4.00%，當次升息已
大致反映於市場預期。我們認為，會後聲明及主席記者會對未來利率政策的態度才是
關鍵：是否暗示還需升息、利率須維持高檔多久，以及是否擔心能源漲價向核心通膨

### Page 4

擴散，並須搭配點陣圖判讀。若後續緊縮訊號未超出預期、長債殖利率趨穩，有利評
價修復；若利率路徑較預期更高、更持久，科技股仍可能承壓。
5. 通膨改善仍不均衡，避免過早判定轉折：8 月核心 CPI 年增率為 2.4%，低於 7 月
的 2.5%，但其月增率由 0.2% 升至 0.3%，顯示年增放緩與短期動能回升並存。後
續須觀察能源漲價是否向服務價格與通膨預期擴散。若油價回穩、核心物價再度降溫
，利率壓力可望緩和；若能源衝擊持續，股市調整時間可能拉長。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦 Fed 決策與 10 月美股財報，科技股中期向上趨勢料不致有變：我們目前仍

維持 AI 核心供應鏈中期正向看法，較看好能將營收成長轉化為獲利與現金流的企業
，但宜密切觀察本週的 FOMC 會議結果與 10 月中將起跑的美股財報。若雲端業者維
持資本支出、晶片公司財測穩定，且 FOMC 此次會議未釋出超預期緊縮訊號，有利
市場信心修復；然而，若出現廣泛支出下修、訂單遞延，或油價與殖利率持續上行，
則應拉長觀察期並重新檢視通膨與利率對於股市動能前景的影響。
2. 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：目前美國的通膨數據仍具韌性，且就業市場
穩健，使年底前市場升息預期再度升溫。市場對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨
仍要求較高風險補償，30 年期殖利率與短天期利差仍維持高檔，顯示期限溢酬尚未收
斂，考量當前 3～10 年期美國公債與投資級債能鎖定目前較高息收，且其受長端財政
風險影響也相對較小，故現階段3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對
較佳。

主因政府支出成長高於收入。9 月單月由去年同期盈餘轉為 269 億美元
赤字，關稅退稅增加壓縮收入，而全年淨利息支出突破1 兆美元、年增
8.4%。在聯邦債務總額逾 40 兆美元下，財政赤字擴大及債務融資成本
攀升，持續考驗財政永續性。 
⚫ 9 月美國成屋銷售年率 399 萬戶，月增 1.5%、年減 2.2%；成屋中位數
售價 41.75 萬美元，年增 1.3%，連續 39 個月上漲。待售庫存增至 160
萬戶 、 供給量4.8 個月 ， 顯示供給改善 ， 惟高利率環境仍抑制購屋需求 。 
⚫ 9 月美國消費者物價指數 (CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.6%，高於 8 月之
0.4%，年增率由 3.4%升至 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，年增率由 2.4%
升至 2.6%。受能源價格上揚影響，能源指數月增 5.2%，其中汽油價格
大幅上漲，成為推升通膨之主要因素；住房指數續增0.4%。整體而言，
美國通膨壓力較前月升溫，核心物價漲勢亦略有增強。 
⚫ 9 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)月增 1.3%，年增率由 5.6%升至 6.3%；能
源價格大漲 10.8%為主要推升因素 ， 帶動商品價格月增2.6%。 服務價格
亦月增 0.8%，核心 PPI 年增率由 4.8%升至 5.0%，顯示生產端通膨壓力
持續升高。

一、 美國—內需與房市提供支撐 美國財政及通膨風險升高 
2026 年 9 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節性調整後為 7,450 億美元，較
上月增加 0.8%，年增 6.3%。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增 0.9%；進一步
扣除汽車、零件及加油站後，核心零售銷售月增0.2%，顯示整體消費需求仍維持
成長。從主要業別表現觀察，加油站、非實體零售商及餐飲服務與飲酒場所銷售
額均較上月增加，其中加油站及餐飲消費表現相對亮眼，電子及家電商店銷售亦
呈成長。整體而言，9 月美國零售銷售延續擴張走勢，顯示民間消費仍具支撐力
道，且多數主要消費類別維持正成長，惟核心零售增幅較前月放緩，後續仍須關
注物價、就業及金融情勢變化對消費支出的影響。 
2026 年 9 月(2026 財年第 12 個月)美國聯邦政府財政赤字為 269.0 億美元 ， 較
去年同期 1,979.4 億美元財政盈餘轉為赤字。當月政府收入 5,578.8 億美元，年增
2.6%；政府支出 5,847.8 億美元，年增 69.1%，支出增幅明顯高於收入。收入方
面，9 月關稅淨收入由 8 月的 128.4 億美元降至 59.1 億美元，累計2026 財年關稅
淨收入約 1,732.2 億美元，年減11.1%，關稅退稅增加已部分抵銷關稅收入成長。
支出方面，9 月聯邦政府淨利息支出約 347.9 億美元，全年累計達1 兆 517.5 億美
元 ， 年增8.4%， 反映高利率環境下債務利息負擔持續攀升 。 累計2026 財年全年 ，
美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1 兆 9,924.7 億美元，較 2025 財年擴大 12.2%。其中，
政府收入年增 3.2%至 5 兆 4,033.3 億美元 ， 支出年增5.5%至 7 兆 3,958.0 億美元 ，
顯示支出成長仍快於收入增加。從支出結構觀察，社會安全、醫療保險、國防及
淨利息支出均較上年增加，持續推升財政壓力。整體而言，美國財政赤字擴大主
要反映政府支出成長強勁、利息負擔居高不下，以及關稅退稅增加削弱部分收入
效果 。 在聯邦債務總額突破40 兆美元背景下 ， 未來財政赤字走勢 、 債務融資成本
及財政永續性問題，仍將是市場關注的重要焦點。 
2026 年 9 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率升至 399 萬戶 ， 較上月增加1.5%，
惟較去年同期減少 2.2%，整體房市成交動能仍偏疲弱。區域表現方面，南部及西
部分別月增 2.8%，中西部持平，東北部月減 2.1%，四大地區銷售量均較去年同
期下滑。房價方面，成屋售價中位數為 41.75 萬美元，年增 1.3%，已連續 39 個

明顯回升，主要受汽油價格大幅上漲及住房成本續增帶動；核心 CPI 月增率維持
0.3%，年增率則回升至 2.6%，顯示核心物價壓力較前月略有增強，未來通膨走勢
仍須持續關注能源價格變化及住房等服務項目價格表現。 
 2026 年 9 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 1.3%，高於上月之
0.5%，年增率則由 5.6%升至 6.3%。其中，最終需求商品價格月增 2.6%，主要受
能源價格月增 10.8%帶動，以汽油、柴油及航空燃油價格漲幅較為顯著；食品價
格亦月增 1.2%。另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.8%，主要反映批發及零售
利潤率、運輸服務價格上升。剔除食品、能源及貿易服務後之核心最終需求 PPI
月增 0.5%，高於上月之 0.3%，年增率由 4.8%升至 5.0%。整體而言，9 月 PPI 升
幅明顯擴大，主要反映能源與服務價格同步上揚，顯示生產端成本壓力仍偏高，
後續仍須關注能源價格及運輸成本變化對通膨走勢之影響。

通膨預期持續惡化，聯準會可能必須採取更積極的緊縮措施，以維持物價穩定。

制對美墨加三國零組件價值的認列範圍；惟美方仍須考量國內金屬產業對降稅可
能衝擊本土生產及就業的疑慮。儘管雙方對關稅調整幅度仍存分歧，墨西哥經貿
官員表示協商已有部分進展，美國商務部亦強調持續與墨方保持溝通，顯示談判
尚未正式破局。整體而言，美墨關稅協商結果不僅攸關北美汽車及金屬產業供應
鏈成本與競爭力，也可能影響 USMCA 後續檢討與延長協商進程；若雙方遲遲無
法縮小分歧，恐增加企業投資與供應鏈布局的不確定性，並對北美區域貿易整合
形成壓力。 
二、歐元區—工業生產走疲，前景有待觀察 
 歐元區 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)較 7 月下滑 0.5%，呈現連續三個月下降，
其中非耐久消費財、中間財及能源產出分別減少 2.7%、0.8%、0.6%，僅資本財、
耐久消費財產出分別成長 0.4%、0.2%。儘管德國 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)反彈
0.4%，係受惠車廠陸續結束歲修，但法、義、西工業生產分別下跌0.3%、1.3%、
0.8%為主要拖累因素，反映先前美伊戰爭帶來的庫存儲備需求逐漸消退，尤其以
能源密集產業(化工、造紙、 鋼鐵等)變化最為明顯。整體而言，即便排除能源密集
產業的波動，其他製造業諸如製藥、汽車、機械等生產情形皆偏疲軟。

三、日本—AI 投資續強，帶動機械訂單穩健復甦 
 8 月核心機械訂單(扣除波動訂單)月增 5.2%，其中製造業訂單月增 1.6%，人

四、中國—AI 相關需求帶動出口動能強勁成長 
9 月出口年增率較 8 月大幅增加 11.3 個百分點至 36.3%，出口總值為 4,472
億美元 ； 累計前九個月出口年增率上升為21.3%， 累積出口總值為33,707 億美元 。
前九個月出口主要成長項目(累積年增率)包含 IC(108.1%)、自動數據處理設備(包
含伺服器等，54.6%)、船舶(29.9%)、汽車(54.3%)，其中 IC 及自動數據處理設備
出口加速成長，為 9 月出口年增率大幅上升的主要因素。 
9 月進口年增率較 8 月增 2.8 個百分點至 31%，進口總值為 3,137 億美元；累
計前九個月進口值成長 27.5%至 24,326 億美元。其中 IC、自動數據處理設備(包
含伺服器等)進口分別成長 63.5%、124.8%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部
分伺服器關鍵組件有賴於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前九個月貿易順差達
9,381 億美元，僅較去年同期增加 7.7%，增幅明顯低於出口，顯示出口擴張同時
伴隨關鍵原物料進口大幅增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價
值產品依賴國際供應鏈支持。

升，其影響逐漸反映於上游生產成本。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格年增率
續揚 14.1 個百分點至 24.5%，並帶動燃料加工業出廠價格年增率上揚至 21.6%，
此外，對於原油價格波動較為敏感的化學品、化纖出廠價格年增率加速升至
12.1%、12.3%，顯示原油價格上漲的影響開始擴散至中下游產業。此外，由於近
期鋼鐵價格走弱 ， 黑色金屬礦產開採 、 冶煉加工業出廠價格分別年減3.4%、0.2%，

9 月消費者通膨(CPI)升 0.1 個百分點為 0.9%，但仍然是今年以來第三低的水
準，核心通膨(不含食品及能源)持平於 1 %不變。該月燃油通膨續增 6.6 個百分點
至 14.9%，在客運成本增加之下，旅遊服務通膨亦增加 0.8 個百分點至 3.5%，此
外，由於手機價格上漲，通信工具通膨上揚 3.7 個百分點至 14.3%；其他商品及
服務價格則大致平穩。整體而言，雖然企業生產成本持續上升，但在民間消費乏
力之下，企業仍大多自行吸收成本，加上政府壓抑零售燃油及民生水電，故目前
原油價格高漲的影響尚未反映於消費端價格。惟考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，
若油價維持高檔或進一步上漲，未來通膨仍有進一步上升的風險。 
展望未來，歐中談判已就汽車貿易達成部分共識，包含未來中國出口至歐盟
的混合動力汽車設定每年 35 萬輛的配額上限，且配額規模可逐年增加 6%。依照
歐盟預估 2026 年自中國進口約 80 萬輛混合動力汽車推算，中國對歐盟的混合動
力車出口量將較原先潛在規模減少一半以上。雖該協議不利於汽車出口，但有助
於避免歐盟進一步擴大貿易保護措施，中國車廠則可能會加速透過赴歐投資設
廠、提升當地生產比重等方式因應配額限制，藉此維持在歐洲市場的競爭力與市
占率。 
五、印度及東南亞—IMF 上調印度及東協的經濟展望；新、馬第三季 GDP 均逾
5% 
(1)據 IMF2026 年 10 月經濟展望報告(WEO)(詳下表)，印度仍是全球主要經濟體
中成長最快者之一，雖然面對油價上漲及全球不確定性，但內需及民間消費韌性
強勁，服務業持續擴張，2026、2027 年 GDP 成長率預估皆為 7.0%。東協國家方
面，受惠於 AI 伺服器與資料中心投資、科技產業供應鏈重組、美中貿易重組帶
來轉單效應等利多，越南、馬來西亞、新加坡、菲律賓及泰國均為 AI 供應鏈受益
國，但須留意地緣政治、全球貿易重組及 AI 投資循環變化，帶來的通膨及經濟
下行風險，建議各國政府提升農業生產效率及糧食安全，提升數位經濟及服務業
競爭力，強化財政紀律並提高公共投資效率，維持穩健貨幣政策與匯率穩定。

反映地緣政治衝擊與惡劣氣候的影響，食品價格較去年同期上漲 7.74%，同時，
油價上漲和盧比貶值推動交通運輸通膨率升至 5.01%，餐飲和住宿 （9.36%） 以及
個人照護和社會安全 （13.75%） 的價格漲幅亦有所上升。9 月批發價格(WPI)大增
11.39%，亦受燃料(25.85%)、製造業(9.76%)和食品(8.66%)價格加速上漲所推動。
由於通膨持續上揚至印度央行 6%的目標上限，恐加大後續升息壓力。 
(3)新加坡：2026 年第三季經濟成長(GDP)季增 1.1%、年增 5.4%，雖較上季增速
放緩，仍為近年較高的成長增速。外需仍為主要成長引擎，全球 AI 相關設備需
求持續擴張，帶動電子產品與機械設備出口成長，加上航空與航運活動活絡，使
貨物及服務出口表現亮眼；相較之下，內需成長較為溫和，零售消費及部分民生
服務需求仍相對疲弱。從產業面分析，製造業年增7.5%，雖較前季放緩，仍是經
濟成長的重要支柱；營建業受公共及民間工程推進成長加速 8.8%；服務業成長
5.2%，以批發貿易受電子出口帶動表現尤佳。IMF 預期新加坡將持續受惠於 AI、
生技及金融服務發展，預估 2026 年經濟成長達 5.0%、2027 年放緩至 3.0%。 
新加坡金融管理局 10 月政策評估維持新元名目有效匯率政策區間不變，反映 4
月和 7 月提前收緊貨幣政策後，核心通膨符合預期，並預計 2027 年年中隨著供
應驅動型進口通膨放緩和國內 生產成本維持低位 ，核心通膨將明顯回落 ，預計
2026 年核心通膨率平均為 1.5%至 2.0%，2027 年則為 1.5%至 2.5%。MAS 表示目
前的升值政策將使新元名目有效匯率持續走強，並抑制近期輸入型通膨的上升，

(4)馬來西亞：2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 5.3%，其中，民間消費受就業市
場穩定、服務業持續擴張及家庭支出成長帶動而維持韌性；投資則受企業擴產及
資料中心等大型專案推進支撐；此外，全球 AI 及科技產業需求強勁，帶動電子
電機產品出口表現，成為外需的重要動能。從產業面分析，製造業成長 加速至
7.5%，為主要成長引擎，係受惠電子、電機及石化相關產業出口增加；服務業、
營建業成長略放慢至 5.4%、6.0%；農業因棕櫚油和畜牧業產量增加從萎縮 3.7%
反彈回升 1.3%， 礦業及採石業因原油與凝析油產量下降而衰退4.9%。 整體而言 ，
馬來西亞經濟呈現「內需穩健、科技出口強勁、投資活絡」的成長格局，IMF 最
新預估 2026 年經濟成長達 5.2%、2027 年放緩至 4.6%。 
六、台灣—AI 帶動出口、投資與消費成長，中經院上修 2026 年經濟預測 
中經院 10 月 16 日公布最新預測，上修 2026 年台灣經濟成長率至 12.16%，
創 1988 年以來新高，主要受惠於 AI 技術快速發展帶動全球科技投資，半導體與
資通訊產品需求強勁，出口維持高速成長，同時民間投資與消費亦同步擴張，經
濟成長動能由內、外需共同支撐。另一方面，由於中東地緣政治風險升溫帶動油
價維持高檔，能源價格外溢效果推升整體物價，中經院將 2026 年 CPI 年增率預
估上修至 2.06%，惟整體通膨仍屬相對溫和。

上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 聯準會上周三決議升息一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，FOMC 政策聲明顯示，18 名
FOMC 成員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次 ， 另有4 人認為存在升息兩次的可
能性 ，加上聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)會後表達控制通膨的偏鷹立場 ， 帶動美
元指數走升至 100 高位。
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數
據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解 、 能源價格持續高位情況下 ， 市場對於歐元區經
濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度。
⚫ 美國公布初請失業金人數為 19.6 萬人，優於市場預期的 20.8 萬，再度呼應聯準
會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)認為目前勞動力市場仍具韌性的說法， 在美國經濟穩
健及通膨頑固的情況下，預計聯準會將持續升息，有利美元持穩。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委暨葡萄牙央行總裁佩雷拉(Alvaro Santos Pereira)表示，歐元區目前

通膨雖未廣泛擴散 ， 惟考量近期天然氣價格飆升 ， 央行將密切關注未來通膨壓力
擴散情形。最新利率期貨市場顯示，歐洲央行今年底仍有再升息一碼空間。
⚫ 日本政府通過消費稅減稅及家庭現金補貼的政策大綱，預計自明年 4 月起的兩
年內，將食品消費稅率自 8%降至 1%，惟大綱中未明確說明資金來源，再度引
發市場對於日本政府的財政擔憂，短線不利日圓表現。
⚫ 台灣央行 9/17 宣布重貼現率連 10 凍維持在 2%不變，央行總裁楊金龍表示，台
灣的通膨相較其他主要經濟體溫和，採取漸進緊縮貨幣政策有助物價穩定。 此
外，目前通膨率為 2%，明年有望降至 2%以下，後續將觀察通膨發展、主要經
濟體貨幣政策緊縮程度及台灣金融情勢，以決定未來貨幣政策走向。

 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.650，低點在 31.958，以 31.808 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶 ， 美國CPI 數據公布後加強市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期 ，
早盤台幣跳空開低，外資連續三日賣超台股並匯出，加以出口商態度轉為惜售，令
台幣接連貶破 31.700、31.800、31.900 關卡， 週四由於聯準會偏鷹派升息，美元指
數走升至 100.300，早盤台幣跳空開低，台幣最低貶至31.958，不過隨後出口商賣壓
出籠，外資雙向進出，台幣收復部分跌幅，維持在31.900 附近震盪；臨近周末，外
資買超台股並於外匯市場匯入 ， 台幣走升至31.722， 最終在央行調節之下收在31.808。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場而言 ， 美國最新初領失業救濟金人數較預
期減少一萬多人、續領失業救濟金人數亦下降，顯示美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，加
以聯準會升息抗通膨，鷹派立場給予美元指數支撐、不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來
看，九月台灣央行並未升息，台美利差擴大預計將帶給台幣壓力，此外，儘管聯準
會升息利空出盡，外資買超台股帶動台幣走升，惟台灣即將迎來中秋連假，預計金
融市場交投謹慎，將令台幣陷入橫盤整理格局，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間
震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

點，帶動人民幣緩步走升，USD/CNH 升破 6.7000 整數大關，終場境外人民幣收在
6.6944，週線升值 94 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6984，週線升值 21 點。

示，聯準會為明年繼續收緊貨幣政策敞開了大門，美元指數走升，抑制人民幣升值
的速度；就中國國內市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實
質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現； 最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 匯價維持
在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.7000 整數關
卡，顯示 USD/CNH 空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪走升。

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走貶。 週初，美國通膨數據維持高位 ，令聯準會升息預期升溫 ，USD/JPY
自 153.29 低點反彈至 155 附近；週間 ，美國聯準會宣布升息一碼 ， 帶動USD/JPY 上
漲至 156.41；臨近週末，日本央行如市場預期升息一碼，惟央行偏鴿派立場令日圓
續貶，帶動 USD/JPY 一度最高來到 158.05，而隨後在日本政府於外匯市場進行匯率
詢價下，USD/JPY 回吐部分漲幅，終場收在 156.86，週線上漲 349 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本政府上周通過消費稅減
稅及家庭現金補貼政策大綱，惟大綱中未明確說明財源，再度引發市場對於日本政
府財政惡化的擔憂，預計將不利日圓走勢。二、日本央行上周五決議將政策利率調
升一碼至 1.25%， 9 名決策委員中有 2 人對升息持反對意見，降低市場對日本央行
進一步升息的預期。另一方面 ， 聯準會上周三亦升息一碼至3.75%-4.00%區間 ，FOMC
政策聲明顯示，18 位官員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次，另有 4 人認為存在升
息兩次的可能性，聯準會升息立場明顯較日本央行更為鷹派，預計將支撐USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月近期再度重申，將於必要時採取外匯干預行動，
以維護外匯市場有序運行。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 短中天期均
線向下發散走勢偏空，惟近期匯價在多次向下測試 154 均拉回，明顯下方有買盤支
撐。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，聯準會升息預期升溫加上歐元區 9 月經濟景氣指
數下滑，令歐元走跌至 1.1525；週間，美國聯準會鷹派升息立場帶動美元走升，歐
元最低來到 1.1460；臨近週末，歐元持續偏弱整理，終場收在 1.1483，週線下跌 107
點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、 歐洲央行管委卡薩克斯
(Martins Kazaks) 表示 ， 為避免美伊戰爭引發的燃料成本上漲開始傳導至工資和其他
商品價格，歐洲央行可能需要漸進式調升利率以抑制通膨。最新利率期貨市場顯示
歐洲央行今年仍有升息一碼的空間，預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、美國聯準會決議將
利率調升一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，聯準會主席華許會後偏鷹言論帶動美元指數走
升至 100 上方，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。三、歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自
前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解、能源
價格持續高位情況下，市場對於歐元區經濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度，將不利歐元
表現。四、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 近期回測年線未能有效突破後明顯
拉回轉為下行走勢，目前匯價承壓於各天期均線下方，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1350-1.1590

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週央行理監事會議決議結果 ， 維持政策利率不變 ， 符合
市場預期 ， 且近兩週央行持續減發定存單 ， 令市場短錢充裕 ， 惟週五銀行間分
配略為不均，部分銀行有資金需求，因此短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.70%，利率高位較上週上升 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投清淡，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三等殖系統成交在 1.86%，10 年
期指標券 A15106 則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.92~1.921%。 
台灣央行理監事會議如市場預期維持政策利率不變 ， 總裁楊金龍表示 ， 今年國
內通膨仍屬可控，明年有望回落至 2%以下，市場對今年升息疑慮有所降溫，
對台債形成支撐 ， 惟國際債市走勢仍偏空 ， 預期台債殖利率短線仍呈高檔區間
盤整格局，後續關注下週三財政部公布的第四季公債發行計劃，預估 5 年券
區間為 1.79%~1.83%，10 年券區間為 1.90%~1.95%。

### Page 1

Unimicron

Extending capacity plan for ABF substrate 
and better margins in PCB/HDI; Buy

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 1,650 TWD | Price: 1,270 TWD

Raise PO along with the hike in estimates 
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 5%/12%/10% after baking in a strengthened margin 
profile, partly thanks to further improved GM (gross margin) in PCB (printed circuit 
board) and HDI (high density interconnect) business. Our PO goes to NT$1,650 (was 
NT$1,400) same based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. Reiterate Buy on Unimicron in view of 
its structurally growing exposure to AI-related (artificial intelligence) products, which in 
turn bolsters its GM profile in longer-term. 
Land acquisition to back long-term capacity growth  
Unimicron announced that it bought a land/building located in Hukuo (Hsinchu) from 
Bridgestone Taiwan, with the amount of NT$10.1bn. Our check with the company 
suggests that such property will be used for further capacity expansion beyond its YM 
(Yangmei) site, but the timing for output will only take place in 2030 at earliest. Of note, 
the company will expand 30+% of its ABF (Ajinomoto build-up film) capacity in 2027 via 
the KF2 (KuangFu 2) plant, and there will be YM3 capacity coming on stream in 2029 
after the expansion YM2 in 2028 backed by 5-year LTA (long-term agreement). For 
2027/28 capex, currently we expect to reach NT$40+bn each year. 
Rosier margin outlook in PCB/HDI business  
Unimicron is one of the major (if not biggest) PCB suppliers to high-end (see report) 
optical module, where demand growth is expected to remain robust coupled with spec 
upgrades in the upcoming years. Management cites such business carries 30+% GM 
currently, while the number by AI HDI in China production lines is moving closer to peers’ 
level. Yet, the PCB/HDI lines in Taiwan still carry lower GM. Currently, we assume its GM 
in HDI business to reach 20+% from mid-2027, along with the continuous yield/output 
improvements.

Company Sector 
Electronics

Company Description 
Unimicron (3037 TT) was founded in 1990 and listed on 
the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 1998. The firm manufactures 
a wide range of PCB and IC substrates for consumer 
electronics, industrial, and automotive. It is also the leading 
ABF supplier globally. The company merged with Phoenix 
Precision Technology (PPT, delisted) in 2009 and expanded 
its exposure in the IC substrate business. UMC is the 
biggest shareholder in Unimicron with a 13.3% stake.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Unimicron in view of strong 
demand from AI accelerators (especially ASICs), which we 
expect to boost the utilization rate of the ABF substrate 
business from 2H26, in turn bolstering the company's 
margin profile and profitability going into 2027.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
Unimicron (UMCRF) 
Our PO of NT$1,650 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E, which is roughly 1 standard deviation 
above the historical average of 20x since 2017. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, 
as we expect Unimicron's earnings to be supported by its high-end technology offerings, 
where end market demand seems more resilient.

Upside risks are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) greater PC and regular server demand, and (3) 
lower competition pressure.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) lower ASP content increase 
at AI server, and (3) bigger drop in the demand of consumer electronic products.

### Page 1

ASE Technology Holding

3Q26 sales outperformance affirms solid 
transformation progress

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 900.00 TWD | Price: 762.00 TWD

3Q26 sales beat on LEAP ramp and mature ASP strength 
ASE reported 3Q26 sales +28% QoQ better than its guide, driven by stronger IC ATM 
(+20% QoQ vs. BofAe +13% QoQ) and mild upside in EMS (+42% QoQ vs. BofAe +40% 
QoQ). We believe IC ATM strength is led by modest mainstream packaging and testing 
price hike on industry supply tightness, supplemented by LEAP business ramp. EMS also 
benefited from Apple product seasonal builds and higher component cost pass through. 
With improving packaging and testing utilization and ASP and rising LEAP mix, we 
expect its IC ATM GMs to improve 300bps QoQ to 30% (vs. high-20% guide) though 
consolidated GMs (+180bps QoQ to 23%) could be partially offset by EMS outgrowth. 
AI pipeline diversification anchors capex plan in ’26-28 
We model ASE’s 4Q26 IC ATM sales +7-9% QoQ as its LEAP opportunity diversifying 
from TSMC outsourcing to direct project wins including AMD’s Venice CPU, putting sales 
on track to +42% YoY in ’26 (vs. its +35% guide). Its IC ATM GMs could further expand 
to 31% as LEAP reaching 30% of its sales (vs. 18%/22% in 1H/3Q26). While we expect 
ASE to spend US$13bn+ annual capex in ’27-28 (vs. US$10.5bn in ’26), the accelerating 
IC ATM sales (BofAe +40% CAGR from ’26-28) on advanced expansion and mature price 
uplift, along with the business mix upgrade to cloud AI/HPC projects carrying 35-45% 
GMs should offset hiking depreciation (BofAe +39% CAGR from ’26-28), lifting its IC 
ATM GM from 29% in ’26 to low 30% in ’27 and mid 30% in ’28 with operating leverage. 
Stronger earnings quality unlocks re-rating potential 
We lift our ’26/27/28E EPS to NT$20/NT$34/NT$50 on strong LEAP outlook. Keep Buy 
rating and raise POs from NT$800 (US$51) to NT$900 (US$58) still on 20x 2H27-1H28 
P/E (vs. its 5-26x range) backed by its structural business transformation with better 
sales and GMs growth profile as the industry supply further consolidates amid higher 
tech entry barrier.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Established in 1984 and headquartered in Taiwan, ASE 
Technology Holding is the no.1 outsourced assembly and 
testing (OSAT) firm globally, in terms of market share by 
revenue. ASE acquired Siliconware Precision (SPIL) in 2018, 
and had acquired USI in 2010, which became its EMS 
business segment providing synergies in system-level 
packaging. 2 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
We rate ASE Buy, factoring in the company's opportunity in 
advanced packaging and testing evidenced in TSMC's 
outsourcing and its own heavy capex investment. The 
strategy shift should allow the company to capture fast 
growing addressable market in high performance 
computing applications and improves its margins. ASE 
could maintain its leading position in the Outsourced 
Semiconductor Assembly and Test (OSAT) universe, owing 
to its well-established footprints and widespread service 
offerings.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
ASE Technology Holding (XSRIF / ASX) 
We value ASE Technology Holding at NT$900 per share (US$58 for ADR), based on 20x 
2H27-1H28E P/E, above its historical range of 5-26x, in view of its solidified industry 
position in AI era. We use P/E ratio to value ASE as the company has been able to 
maintain healthy profitability through the cycles in the past 20 years due to 
consolidating industry landscape supporting a more benign supply/demand and keeping 
pricing and margins less cyclical.

Downside risks to our PO are 1) share loss and/or ASP erosion owing to Chinese players' 
cannibalization and/or ASE's worsening execution, 2) weakening end demand due to 
uncontrollable matters, such as macro halt and/or geopolitical tensions, 3) regulatory 
issues that could hinder ASE from generating synergies and benefit its competitors.

Earnings revision: We factor in WNC 2Q26 result and revise up earnings by 1%/ 2%/ 2% 
in 2026-28E to reflect higher than expected revenues of WiFi 7/ WiFi 8 and switch 
products, driven by spec upgrade and rising client demand. We keep GM and Opex ratio 
largely unchanged.

Valuation: With the earning estimate changes, we raise our 12M TP to NT$380.0 (vs. 
previously at NT$371, based on 28.0x 2027E P/E, unchanged). We continue to base 
our TP on 2027E P/E, and the target multiple is still derived from the correlation 
between P/E and EPS growth of its peers, which is higher as we mark to market. Our 
target P/E of 28.0x is higher than company’s avg. +1 std P/E (16.3x), which we believe is 
not stretched considering the sustainable growth of WNC driven by long-term satellite 
and high speed networking demand. Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology: WNC 
Valuation: We use a near-term P/E to derive our 12m target price for WNC, consistent 
with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our TP of NT$380.0 on a target P/E 
multiple of 28.0x on a forward year EPS (2027E). Our target P/E is derived from the 
correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers. We are Buy rated on WNC. Key 
downside risks: fiercer-than-expected competition in satellite communication, 
slower-than-expected Wi-Fi 7 or 5G FWA ramp-up in US and Europe markets.

and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

耕興 (6146 TT)  Buy

耕興評價已修正，有機會受惠轉單效應，給 Buy 的投資建
議。  
投資建議 ：美國 FCC 有機率禁止，銷往美國的電子產品在中國
實驗室進行測試，耕興有望接受大量轉單，給 Buy 的投資建議。  
公司簡介： 耕興主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規
檢測服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零
組件之銷售，全球知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、
網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一： FCC 公布
1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機 ： 路透社 (Reuters) 報導 ，
FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電子產品，
是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試 ， 若表決通過 ，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利： 耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，
若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室 ， 將會有大量相關訂單轉移至台灣廠商 ，
耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
Environmental ：與 2021 年相比， 2022 年之用電度數較 2021 年減少 0.68% 、
減紙 42%( 減碳 7.92 公噸 CO 2 )。未來規劃：華亞實驗室建置太陽能發電系
統；建置雨水或空調冷凝水回收系統；分階段換發 LED 節能燈具、省電空
調；並於馬達設備增設變頻器以節省用電。  
Social ：體恤員工與資助社團法人嘉義縣扶緣服務協會、新港文教基金會、
與新港奉天宮之金虎爺慢跑活動。國內交易量一百萬元以上的供應商中，
92% 已簽屬「供應商社會責任承諾書」並定期查核；零件部跟生產相關的原
料供應商皆已簽署「不使用衝突礦產聲明書」。  
Governance ：董事會治理制度包含「董事會議事規範」、衡量績效目標落實
之「董事績效評估辦法」、確保誠信經營之「誠信經營守則」、避免貪腐與
符合公平交易法之「誠信經營作業程序及行為指南」等。訂定「資訊安全政
策規範」並以 PDCA (Plan, Do, Check, Act) 循環方式管理。  
公司簡介：  
耕興成立於 1997 年，主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規檢測
服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零組件之銷售，全球
知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
由於耕興具備 GCF 、 PTCRB 組織的符合性檢測認證，以及加拿大與日本發
證權，只要客戶通過公司認證後即可銷往多國，可降低檢驗所需時間與成
本。  
根據 2022 年 FCC 所公布的資料，耕興在 5G/Wi-Fi 的檢測市占率已經是世
界第一，產能也是全球第一。手機 /網通賣的款式越多，對耕興越有利，單
一產品的出貨量對耕興營收無關。  
台灣無線通訊檢測相關同業包含東研信超 (6840 TT) 、穩得 (6761 TT) 和敦吉
(2459 TT) ，耕興的策略是不斷地去投入比同業多的 CAPEX ，做最先進規格
的檢測，像是毫米波、 Wi-Fi 7 的檢測，以提升營收與獲利。  
營收占比 _地區別：  
浩興為耕興的中國子公司。

資料來源：耕興法說簡報。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 14 日

3 群益投顧 
AI 筆電、企業級 AP 檢測需求帶動耕興 1H26 營運成長：  
耕興表示， 1H26 獲利增長主要來自於實體 AI 終端裝置發展同時結合非
MRA(Mutual Recognition Arrangement ，相互承認協議 ) 國家地區 FCC 
PAG(Pre-Approval Guidance) 轉單效應帶動大量高階機種檢測需求。  
耕興內部統計最顯著增長的兩大產業族群來自於 AI 筆電及北美企業級 AP
檢測訂單需求增加。  
2H26 營運有望逐季成長：  
展望 2H26 同樣伴隨 PAG 轉單效應持續消化大量湧入的 AI 筆電及北美企業
級 AP 檢測訂單。檢測需求帶動下， 3Q26 獲利表現將有機會比 2Q26 強勁，
同時 4Q26 的獲利也有機會更上一層樓， 4Q26 是 2027 年高階旗艦級手機種
換機潮的檢測高峰期。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一：  
FCC 公布 1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機：  
路透社 (Reuters) 報導， FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電
子產品，是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試，若表決通過，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
FCC 主席表示， 2025 年賣到美國的電子產品，約有 82% 是在中國實驗室進
行測試，而在美國實驗室測試的比例不到 4%。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利：  
耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室，將會有大量相關訂
單轉移至台灣廠商，耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

HiMSEN 엔진 및 SMR 주기기 생산설비 대규모 증설 발표 
- 9/10 HD현대중공업은 총 1조 722억원 규모의 시설투자 2건을 공시 
① 대출력 발전엔진(4행정 중속엔진, HiMSEN엔진) 생산능력 확충에 8,336
억원 투입, 글로벌 전력수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 확대에 대응하는 목
적. 대출력 발전엔진 전용 공장 신설과 토지 취득, 건축물·기계설비 등 생산설
비 구축이 골자. 투자기간은 ‘26.09~’28.05(설계/인허가 ~’27.02, 공장건설 
~’28.05. ’28.06 상업운전개시, 28년 가동률 30%, 2030년 100% 도달) 
② SMR 주기기 제작 생산시설 구축에 2,386억원(자기자본 대비 2.55%) 투
입, SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주 물량 증가에 대응. SMR 전용 공장 
및 설비 신설과 기존 공장 최적화를 병행, 투자기간은 ’26.10~’29.04. 
기다리던 증설 공시. 늘어난 Capa로 AI DC향 수혜 확대 기대 
- 25년 3.0GW(선박용 2.3GW + 육상용 0.7GW)였던 HiMSEN 엔진 생산 
Capa는 2030년 7.2GW(선박용 3.2GW + 육상용 4.0GW)로 확대. 그간 선
박용 중심이었던 엔진 사업의 포트폴리오를 발전용으로 본격 다각화하는 사
업 재편으로 해석 
- AI DC 신규 증설 확대 추세 속 계통 연결의 어려움과 CCGT, Aero -
derivative 대비 납기 및 부하상승시간, 모듈화 용이성 영역에서 장점을 가진 
발전용 중속엔진에 대한 수요 지속 기대(260908 당사 보고서 참조) 
- 지난 Corban Energy Group 향 계약 건(1,000MW, $ 674 mn)을 단순 
적용 시 금번 증설의 향후 매출액 기여규모는 연간 $ 2.8 bn에 달함. 엔진 시
장의 Seller-Market 기조에 따라 20%를 상회하는 높은 이익률이 지속될 것
으로 전망함에 따라 향후 유의미한 이익 성장 기대 
- HD현대마린솔루션 또한 LTSA 수주 저변 확대 측면에서 향후 이익 확장 가
능. HiMSEN엔진 고유 라이선스 보유에 따른 시너지로 그룹사 전반의 수혜

Document 243: AIと金融市場、強まる相互依存 ～IMFの国際金融安定性報告書から読み解く新たなリスク～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIと金融市場、強まる相互依存 ～IMFの国際金融安定性報告書から読み解く新たなリスク～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026 年10 月 15 日

1/5 
AI と金融市場、強まる相互依存 
～IMF の国際金融安定性報告書から読み解く新たなリスク～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

1. はじめに 
国際通貨基金（IMF）は10 月 13 日、国際金融安定性報告書（Global Financial Stability 
Report、GFSR）の全体版を公表した。金融市場は底堅さを維持していると評価したうえで、金融
安定上のリスクは高いとした。主なリスクとして①人工知能（AI）関連資産の大幅な値下がり、
②国債利回りの急上昇、③サイバー攻撃の三つを取り上げた。 
GFSRは世界経済の動向を分析する「世界経済見通し」 （WEO）と並ぶIMFの主要な定期報告書だ。
株式・債券市場や銀行、ノンバンクなどを幅広く点検し、金融システムの安定を損なうリスクや
ショックを増幅させる脆弱性を分析する。各国の金融当局や市場関係者にとって、その時々の金
融市場・システムの課題を示すものとして注目度が高い。本稿では、GFSRが今回示した三つのリ
スクを整理する（資料1）。特にAIが金融市場・システムにもたらす影響を詳しくみていく。

資料 1 GFSR（2026 年 10 月版）があげた三つのリスク

（出所）IMF「GFSR（2026 年 10 月）」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成 
リスク 評価の変化 評価の概要
AI関連資産
の大幅な値
下がり
横ばい
引き金：AI関連投資の規模や持続性、収益性が期待を下回る。
評価：AI関連資産の規模が拡大し ているため、影響は大きくな
り得る。中核的なAI関連企業の収益は好調で、株価の割高感は
和らいだ一方、AI関連分野でレバレッジが拡大し ている。
国債利回り
の急上昇
悪化
引き金：インフレ再燃、財政問題でタームプレミア ムが上昇。
評価：国債市場は金融市場の基盤で影響は深刻になり得る。高
水準の政府債務や借り換えリスクは未解決のため、財政・イン
フレのショックをより頻繁に受けやすい。
サイバー攻
撃
悪化
引き金：重要な金融機関や市場インフラがサイバー攻撃で被害
評価：デジタル化の急速な進展で攻撃対象が広がり、影響は深
刻になり得る。最先端のAIモデルは攻撃と防御の双方に利用さ
れる可能性。

### Page 2

2/5 
2. AI関連資産の大幅な値下がりリスク  
一つ目のリスクにあげたのはAI関連資産の大幅な値下がりだ。4 月版からの変化を「横ばい」と
評価した。しかし、これは状況が変わっていないという意味ではない。AI関連の中核企業が高い
利益成長を続け、株価の割高感が薄れた点を評価した一方、巨額の設備投資によるフリーキャッ
シュフローの減少や社債発行の増加、投資家によるレバレッジ拡大を警戒している。 
IMFは、AI関連投資を世界経済や金融市場の重要な下支え要因と評価している。生成AIなどの普
及に伴い、データセンターや半導体投資は活発で、関連企業の業績は好調だ。AI関連企業の株価
が急上昇した一方、予想利益も大きく伸びている。GFSRは、一部の株式市場で予想利益の伸びが
株価上昇を上回り、予想株価収益率（PER）が低下した点を評価している。 
一方、リスクが高まっている点として注目するのは、大規模なクラウドサービスやデータセン
ターを運営する企業（ハイパースケーラー）のフリーキャッシュフロー（事業で稼いだ現金から
設備投資などを差し引いた額）の減少だ。設備投資の急増により、資金調達のため社債発行が拡
大している。IMFによると、世界の投資適格社債発行額に占めるハイパースケーラーの割合は、
2024 年の1％未満から2026 年には約10％に上昇した（資料2）。社債の信用スプレッドも、4 月版
GFSRの公表以降、投資適格社債市場全体と比べて約30 ベーシスポイント拡大したという（注 1）。

資料 2 世界の投資適格社債発行額に占めるハイパースケーラーの割合

（出所）IMF「GFSR（2026 年 10 月）」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

現在のAI関連企業の資金調達の特徴として、資金と売上が企業間で相互に結びつく循環的な取
引があげられる。具体的には、大手企業が財務基盤の相対的に弱いAI企業に出資し、出資先がそ

### Page 3

3/5 
の資金で大手企業のサービスや半導体を購入する仕組みだ。GFSRによれば、2023 年以降に公表さ
れた 14 件の取引では、株式投資が約3,000 億ドル、関連する債務が約6,500 億ドルにのぼり、将
来の売上に関する契約は約1 兆 4,000 億ドルに達する。こうした取引はうまくいけば、出資先の
企業価値拡大による投資リターンの増加と売上増加が期待できる。一方、投資先の経営が悪化す
れば、投資損失と将来の売上減少という逆回転が生じる。IMFは、こうした取引による投資資金や
将来の売上が少数の企業に集中しており、特定の投資先の経営悪化がAI関連企業の間で損失を広
げるリスクを指摘している。 
AI関連企業に投資する側の懸念としてGFSRが取り上げたのは、株価上昇が続くなか、借り入れ
などを利用した取引が拡大し、投資家が過大なリスクを抱えている可能性だ。ヘッジファンドの
株式ポジションと信用取引融資残高の合計は、2024 年末の先進国GDP比約10％から2026 年には約
15％に増加した。また、レバレッジ型上場投資信託（ETF）に占める個別株を対象としたETFの割
合は、16％から28％に上昇した。GFSRの補足分析によると、株式オプションの価格から推計した
レバレッジ取引の資金調達コストも、2026 年初めからほぼ倍増している（注 2）。レバレッジをか
けた取引は株価が下落した際の損失が大きくなり、追加証拠金を拠出するために他の資産を売ら
ざるを得なくなるといった形で、他の金融商品にも波及する場合がある。

3. 国債利回りの急上昇リスク  
AIと同様に金融市場で注目が高まっている国債について、GFSRは利回りの急上昇リスクを4 月
版から「悪化」と評価した。特に注目したいのは、長期国債を保有するリスクに応じて投資家が
求める上乗せ金利（タームプレミアム）の上昇だ。IMFによると、10 年国債利回りの上昇には将来
の政策金利の予想とタームプレミアムの双方が寄与しており、特に先進国のタームプレミアムは
歴史的な変動範囲の上限に近い水準にある。 
タームプレミアムを押し上げているのは、財政赤字の継続や高水準の政府債務に対する懸念、
今後の国債発行増加の見通しだ。ユーロ圏、英国、米国では、2026 年1～3 月期に投資適格社債の
発行が国債を含む債券発行総額の4 割を占めた（デュレーション加重） 。さらにGFSRは、AI関連企
業による社債発行が急増し、国債と投資家の資金を奪い合うことで、国債市場への追加的な圧力
になっているとの指摘も紹介している。 
IMFが警戒するのは、インフレ再燃などをきっかけにタームプレミアムがさらに上昇する事態
だ。主要国では、政府債務が歴史的に高い水準にあるという問題が未解決のままで、国債の借り
換えリスクも高い。特に、国債の償還年限を短期化した国では、金利が上昇すると高い金利で借
り換える必要が生じ、財政負担が増える。長期債から短期債への置き換えが大規模に進めば、こ
うしたリスクは一段と高まる可能性がある。 
政府債務が増えて国債発行が膨らむと、国内の銀行や保険会社などだけでは増発分を消化しき
れず、外国人投資家や投資ファンドの存在感が高まりやすい。こうした投資家は価格変動に敏感
で、インフレ懸念が強まった際に長期国債の売却が集中する可能性がある。GFSRでは、これをタ
ームプレミアムの上昇を増幅する要因としてあげている。

### Page 4

4/5 
GFSRは、日本国債市場の流動性悪化を指摘している。日本銀行によるイールドカーブ・コント
ロールの終了後、金融政策の正常化が進むなかで利回りの変動が大きくなったことに加え、特に
長期国債で売値と買値の差が拡大している点をあげた。ユーロ圏と英国では流動性を示す指標が
改善し、米国でも足元は良好な状況にあるのとは対照的だ。 
IMFはタームプレミアムが急上昇した場合、AI関連株を含む幅広い資産の価格が下落するほか、
金融取引に利用される国債の担保価値も低下し、金融市場全体に混乱が広がる可能性を警戒して
いる。IMFは世界各国の銀行を対象に、国債利回りの急上昇やAIの生産性向上効果が期待を下回る
ことなどを想定したストレステストを実施した。この結果、世界全体の銀行システムの自己資本
比率は規制上の最低水準を上回るものの、ストレス後の普通株式等Tier1 比率が 7％を下回る銀行
の資産は、世界の銀行資産全体の22％を占める結果になった（注3）。

4. サイバー攻撃のリスク 
GFSRは、三つ目のリスクとしてあげたサイバーリスクについて、「悪化」したと評価した。GFSR
では金融機関が外部の情報技術サービスへの依存を強めている点を問題視した。クラウドサービ
スやソフトウエアを提供する企業の一部に利用が集中し、多くの金融機関が共通の技術基盤を利
用するようになっている。こうした企業が攻撃を受けてサービスを停止すれば、金融機関への影
響が広範に及ぶ可能性がある。さらに、外部委託先のつながりが複雑になり、リスクの全体像を
把握しにくくなっている点も指摘した。 
サイバー攻撃は高度化が進み、日本でも企業の情報流出が相次ぐ。GFSRは、最先端のAIモデル
がネットワーク攻撃の模擬実験で高い能力を示していると指摘した。GFSRによると、悪用が確認
されたソフトウエアなどの脆弱性のうち、公表日以前または公表当日に悪用された割合（ゼロデ
イ比率）は、2026 年の年初来で70％を超え、前年から約20 ポイント上昇した。脆弱性が公表さ
れてから悪用されるまでの時間も短くなっており、ソフトウエアの提供企業が修正プログラムを
用意する余裕は小さくなっている。

5. おわりに 
今回のGFSRからは、AIの急成長に金融市場や金融システムがどこまで対応できるのかという問
題意識が垣間見える。AI関連企業の利益成長が世界的な株高をけん引する一方、巨額の設備投資
に必要な資金を調達するため、社債市場でもその存在感を高め始めた。技術革新が進むほど投資
資金への需要も膨らみ、あらゆる市場から資金を吸い上げる構図だ。 
成長分野に資金が集中し、市場が過熱することは過去にも繰り返されてきた。今回のAIブーム
で特徴的なのは、巨額の資金需要が国債市場の需給にまで影響を及ぼす可能性があることだ。AI
の成長が金融市場を動かす一方、金利の上昇が今度はAI投資の採算を左右する。相互依存の強ま
りが今回のGFSRからも確認できたといえる。 
以 上

### Page 5

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
5/5
【注釈】
1) IMF「Global Financial Stability Report」（2026 年 10 月版） 、第1 章補足分析1.1「Rising
Leverage in the AI Ecosystem」3 頁。
2) 上記補足分析1.1、5～6 頁、図 1.1.4。資金調達コストは、S&P500 の株式オプション価格から
推計した担保付翌日物調達金利に対する30 日間の上乗せ金利。
3) IMF「Global Financial Stability Report」（2026 年 10 月版） 、第1 章 7～9 頁、図1.7 および
補足分析1.2「2026 IMF Global Banking Stress Test」。ストレステストの対象は29 カ国の

665 銀行。

统避险资产集中的态势明显。

度的⽅向性⾛势即将到来。
" 在某种程度上，当前价格⾛势的盘旋特征暗⽰突破在即， "BMO 策略师在报告中写道。

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

The bank
for a changing

EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Vulnerabilities differ, depending on a country’s dependence on food, energy and logistics

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.
helene.drouot@bnpparibas.com

Raissi

Document 406: Top of Mind - Higher Bond Yields_ How Concerning_

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the r ails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, pr ovided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor  
Email: allison.nathan@gs.com     
Tel: 212-357-7504   
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC

Higher bond yields: how concerning?

### Page 4

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 4 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Mark Zandi is Chief Economist of Moody’s Analytics. Below, he argues that the US economy 
is vulnerable rather than resilient and worries that high interest rates could deepen existing 
pockets of weakness and ultimately cause something to break in the financial system.   
The views stated herein are those of the interviewee and do not necessarily reflect those of Goldman Sachs.
Allison Nathan: Prior to the 
September 2026 Fed rate hike, you 
argued that a hike would do little to 
address supply-driven inflation but 
could damage already weak parts of 
the US economy. Where are you 
seeing the most softness? 
Mark Zandi: The clearest softness is 
in the labor market. Monthly payroll 
growth has averaged around 40,000 over the past year and has 
been essentially zero when excluding the healthcare sector. 
Hiring is moribund, hours worked are very low, and while the 
unemployment rate remains low, that is partly a reflection of 
declining labor force participation. Adjusting for that cyclical 
drop would put underlying unemployment in the mid-4% 
range—above the level consistent with full employment—and I 
expect the headline rate to move toward that level over the 
next 6-18 months. Real wage growth is, at best, flat, and for 
many Americans is actually declining, which is eroding 
purchasing power, particularly for lower- and middle-income 
households, who are increasingly drawing down savings to 
sustain spending. So, while the economy is still growing, the 
underlying labor market and household fundamentals point to 
very tenuous growth. 
 While the economy is still growing, the 
underlying labor market and household 
fundamentals point to very tenuous growth.”   
Allison Nathan: What is causing this softness? 
Mark Zandi: Policy choices are at the root of the weakness. 
Tariffs, while more consequential last year, continue to weigh 
on growth and boost inflation. Heavy-handed immigration policy 
is also an underappreciated source of weakness. Many firms 
losing immigrant workers cannot afford to raise wages enough 
to attract native-born workers, so they are raising prices to align 
demand with the supply they can produce with a reduced labor 
force. But the most immediate and significant driver of the 
softness is the Iran war, which has pushed up energy prices, 
further fueling inflation and weighing on growth.  
Allison Nathan: But US real GDP growth remains near 2%—
roughly the economy’s potential rate. Isn’t that a relatively 
healthy growth pace? 
Mark Zandi: Real GDP growth is indeed running at 2% yoy, but 
I estimate that AI-related activity accounts for roughly one-third 
of that, or around 0.6–0.7pp. Roughly half of that comes from AI 
capital spending, net of electronics imports for data centers, 
while the balance reflects wealth effects from the surge in AI-
related stock prices that have contributed to an almost doubling 
in the S&P 500 since ChatGPT debuted four years ago, boosting 
net worth and consumer spending, especially among high-
income households. So, the non-AI economy is growing at only 
around 1%–1.5%. That’s a pretty punk economy, and a difficult 
place to be. 
Allison Nathan: The September SEP indicated that most 
FOMC members expect one more rate hike this year, and 
the market is expecting the same. How damaging could 
further tightening be? 
Mark Zandi: While I expect another hike this year, with the 
economy already barely growing at potential, additional hikes 
could push growth below potential. That would further strain 
the already weak non-AI economy and trigger layoffs, 
significantly raising the likelihood of a self-reinforcing negative 
spiral. So, further tightening would be a risky endeavor. 
 With the economy already barely 
growing at potential, additional hikes could 
push growth below potential. That would 
further strain the already weak non-AI 
economy and trigger layoffs, significantly 
raising the likelihood of a self-reinforcing 
negative spiral.”   
Higher rates are already inflicting damage on the economy’s 
most rate-sensitive sectors, particularly housing and 
commercial real estate. Refinancing is in a deep freeze. Existing 
home sales have fallen to levels last seen during the teeth of 
the housing crash and the Global Financial Crisis. Home prices 
are moving sideways on average and outright declining in half 
the country. That could easily create broader problems for 
households, especially for those that bought homes in the last 
years after rates began to rise and have virtually no home 
equity. And while commercial real estate had begun to show 
signs of life, renewed rate increases are once again freezing 
activity and putting pressure on property values.  
Higher rates are also squeezing existing debtors as borrowing 
costs on credit cards, home loans, auto loans, and corporate 
debt rise. I am particularly worried about highly leveraged, often 
private equity-backed companies that have managed the higher 
rate environment by extending maturities and limiting near-term 
interest payments. That strategy cannot continue indefinitely if 
borrowing costs remain elevated or continue to rise.  
The equity market is another key area of concern. AI continues 
to propel the equity market, but it is not impervious to higher 
rates. Persistently elevated rates will eventually weigh on 
valuations, increasing the risk of a major correction that could 
quickly reverberate through the real economy as positive wealth 
effects reverse and consumer spending weakens. AI capex is 
also vulnerable as a higher cost of capital makes investment 
Interview with Mark Zandi

### Page 5

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 5 
Top of Mind Issue 152

more expensive and falling valuations make it harder to raise 
the capital necessary to sustain the current pace of the AI 
buildout. For now, AI is operating under its own momentum, 
supported by considerable optimism and euphoria. But if rates 
remain at these levels or higher for longer than a quarter or two, 
AI could become the part of the economy that breaks. 
Last but not least, higher rates will worsen the US fiscal 
situation, which is itself contributing to elevated yields today. 
While we have navigated this fiscal risk for some time, the 
fiscal situation is as bad as I have ever seen it. The deficit 
exceeds 6% of GDP, the primary deficit is above 3%, and debt 
is roughly 100% of GDP and on track to reach 125% in a 
decade. Annual federal interest payments are already well 
above $1tn and will rise rapidly if rates remain elevated. All told, 
I see a roughly one-in-five chance of a fiscal cliff event over the 
coming year—unlikely, but large enough to warrant attention. 
So, higher rates will weigh on the economy through several 
channels. Historically, rate increases of this magnitude have 
almost invariably caused something to break in the financial 
system. We cannot know exactly where the fault line will 
emerge, but the risk of these pockets of weakness becoming a 
broader problem is significant. 
 I view the distribution of possible 
outcomes as very flat and heavily skewed 
toward darker tail scenarios.”  
Allison Nathan: How do these risks translate into your 
forecasts for economic growth? 
Mark Zandi: My baseline forecast is relatively sanguine based 
on the assumption that the Iran war begins to wind down, 
allowing oil prices to decline to around $80/bbl as we move into 
next year. That should ease inflation, reduce Fed tightening 
concerns, and bring the 10-year Treasury yield back toward 
4.5% by early next year—a level the economy can digest.  
Importantly, though, I view the distribution of possible 
outcomes as very flat and heavily skewed toward darker tail 
scenarios. The war may not fade into the background, keeping 
energy prices elevated and raising the risk of further inflation 
pressure. That could prompt additional Fed tightening, push 
yields even higher, and ultimately cause something to break in 
the financial system.  
Meanwhile, the Treasury market has grown more fragile as 
traditional, price-insensitive buyers—including the Fed, banks, 
and foreign institutional investors—have stepped back, 
increasing the market’s reliance on highly leveraged, price-
sensitive hedge funds that could rush for the exits under stress. 
A debt-limit confrontation under a likely divided US government 
next year could provide the catalyst, particularly if lawmakers 
underestimate the consequences of a breach. In that scenario, 
an even sharper bond market selloff than we are currently 
experiencing could occur. While none of this is my baseline—
and I remain inherently optimistic because avoiding the darkest 
scenarios is largely a policy choice—I put the odds of a severe 
bond market selloff at around one-in-four and of the economy 
going completely off the rails for any of the reasons we have 
discussed at around one-in-three. So, the downside risks are 
significant. 
 I put the odds of a severe bond market 
selloff at around one-in-four and of the 
economy going completely off the rails for 
any of the reasons we have discussed at 
around one-in-three. So, the downside risks 
are significant.”  
Allison Nathan: The Fed has considerable room to cut 
rates. Should that provide any comfort that the worst-case 
scenarios may be avoided? 
Mark Zandi: That remains an open question. If inflation fades 
and allows the Fed to take its foot off the brake, rate cuts 
should bring yields down across the curve—though not back to 
pre-war levels. But in a darker scenario involving a debt limit 
crisis and even higher yields, short-term rate cuts would have 
little effect on the long end. The Fed would need to turn to 
alternative policy tools, including quantitative easing and bond 
purchases. That would amount to financial repression—a 
strategy similar to the one employed by the BoJ for many 
years—which could prove counterproductive by reducing the 
pressure on lawmakers to address the underlying problems 
while fueling even more persistent inflation. 
Allison Nathan: Why do you think others are more 
sanguine about the risks that higher interest rates pose to 
the US economy? 
Mark Zandi: Some likely see greater underlying economic 
strength and expect AI to drive much stronger growth. Others 
may believe that the neutral rate is higher, perhaps for the 
same reasons, while some may be more concerned about 
persistently higher inflation.  
But the more fundamental difference is the broader lens 
through which I currently view the economy. Many continue to 
describe the US economy as resilient, which I find fascinating, 
bordering on frustrating. In my view, the right word is 
vulnerable. Again, growth is pedestrian, the labor market is soft, 
which was further underscored by the much weaker-than- 
expected September jobs report, real wage growth is 
decelerating, inflation remains elevated, and interest rates are 
rising. That shift in perspective completely changes how one 
evaluates the risks ahead.

### Page 6

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 6 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Tobias Adrian is Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former Financial Counsellor and 
Director of the Monetary and Capital Markets Department at the IMF. Below, he argues that 
the global economy has been resilient despite higher bond yields and could very well remain 
so. But he sees several areas of concern in the economy and markets.  
The views stated herein are those of the interviewee and do not necessarily reflect those of Goldman Sachs, the IMF, IMF 
Management, or the IMF Executive Board.
Jenny Grimberg: The IMF has 
cautioned that sharp increases in 
global bond yields could create 
strains across economies and 
markets. How would you assess the 
global economy’s response to the 
recent rise in yields?  
Tobias Adrian: The global economy 
has so far responded to the rise in 
yields much the same way as it has to the shocks of the past 
seven years: resiliently. If somebody had told you in late 2019 
that the global economy would face a pandemic, multiple wars, 
deep geopolitical fragmentation, and trade and energy supply 
shocks, you would have been hard-pressed not to expect a 
global recession. The pandemic did, of course, trigger a 
contraction, but it was exceptionally short-lived despite the 
severe supply and demand shocks. And the global monetary 
tightening cycle that began in 2021 and accelerated sharply in 
2022 in response to the pandemic-related inflation surge did not 
trigger a recession at all, which is unusual by historical 
standards. So, the global economy has proven deeply resilient.   
Jenny Grimberg: What has that resilience owed to? 
Tobias Adrian: Three factors explain this resilience: policy, the 
adaptability of the economy, and some luck related to 
technological innovation. Over the past seven years, fiscal and 
monetary policymakers acted aggressively to stabilize economic 
activity. Fiscal policy provided substantial support during the 
pandemic, while central banks cut interest rates, expanded 
asset purchases, and established emergency standing facilities. 
That fiscal support has of course come at a significant long-term 
cost as debt-to-GDP ratios in advanced economies have surged 
since 2019, and deficits in many countries remain large, 
contributing to the rise in long-term yields. But policy has 
undoubtedly been critical to the economy’s resilience to date.  
The economy itself has also become more adaptable. A 
dividend of the pandemic is that firms have developed a greater 
ability to rewire global value chains quickly in response to a 
range of disruptions, from shifts in energy and food prices to 
geopolitical developments, which has helped the global 
economy more effectively absorb shocks.  
And luck has played a role in that the more recent challenges 
have coincided with the emergence and rapid adoption of 
generative AI technology, which has helped fuel expectations of 
a broad productivity boom and created massive demand for 
investment that is stimulating aggregate demand. If large 
language models (LLMs) had not come to the fore in November 
2022, global growth in the more recent period certainly would 
not have been as strong.  
Jenny Grimberg: As bond yields continue to climb, are you 
concerned that this resilience could falter? 
Tobias Adrian: So far, the rise in bond yields has not triggered 
a substantial, broad-based tightening in financial conditions or, 
more importantly, disruptions to market functioning or forced 
deleveraging that could damage markets and the economy. But 
I see three main areas of concern: the AI and technology 
complex, sovereign bond markets, and emerging markets (EMs).  
Three vulnerabilities stand out in the AI and technology 
complex. First, the interconnectivity and circularity of 
investment among many AI firms raises the risk that one firm’s 
earnings hit could fairly quickly spread across the broader 
complex. Second, many AI firms have taken on significant 
leverage, and investor leverage has also grown through the use 
of leveraged ETFs and derivatives. Leverage upon leverage can 
be problematic when adverse shocks hit. Third, while relatively 
subdued valuations suggest that the market is not in a 1999 
state of indiscriminate price appreciation, with investors 
continuing to differentiate across firms and sectors, the 
strength of the complex is predicated on strong earnings and 
high revenue growth, and it is not clear how long that will last. 
While my baseline outlook is positive, greater competition from 
open-source models, for example, could eat into AI firms’ 
profitability and pressure the broader value chain.    
Sovereign debt markets are another key concern. As I 
mentioned, global public debt is approaching 100% of GDP, and 
debt levels in major advanced economies exceed 120% of 
GDP. Those are staggering figures from a historical perspective. 
The investor base for sovereign bonds has also increasingly 
shifted to more leveraged investors who are more sensitive to 
macro developments. A key indicator to watch is auction tails—
the difference between the yield at which a government sells 
bonds at auction and the yield the market expected beforehand. 
Looking at 30-year bond auctions across G4 sovereign bond 
markets, the tails have grown over time, suggesting increased 
market vulnerability to supply. 5-to-10-year forward term premia 
have risen to pre-Global Financial Crisis (GFC) levels. So, the 
quantitative easing period has now been fully priced out of the 
long end of the global yield curve. And I see no good reason for 
term premia to decline in the foreseeable future. If anything, 
both expected short rates and the term premium will probably 
remain somewhat elevated.  
Finally, while EM economies have shown tremendous 
resilience through the shocks of the past seven years owing to 
stronger policy frameworks, resilient FX reserve buffers, and 
the development of local-currency bond markets that have 
reduced currency mismatches, vulnerabilities remain. 
Particularly striking is the K-shaped pattern of capital flows. 
Capital has flowed into EM sovereign bonds, while equity 
Interview with Tobias Adrian

### Page 7

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 7 
Top of Mind Issue 152

capital has flowed out on net as domestic investors direct their 
savings into global—and, in particular, US—technology stocks. 
So, even though EM equities have rallied over the past two 
years, capital flows have moved in the other direction. That 
leaves these countries’ external positions more leveraged, with 
less equity and more debt on net.  
Jenny Grimberg: Higher yields played a key role in the 2023 
regional banking crisis. Are you worried about banks today? 
Tobias Adrian: Not particularly. Banks generally do well when 
interest rates are elevated, because financial intermediation is 
fundamentally about spreads. When rates rise, asset yields 
often reprice faster than deposit costs, supporting bank net 
revenues. That partly explained the banking system’s overall 
resilience in 2023, even as a few outlier banks ran into trouble. 
Significant capital and liquidity buffers broadly absorbed mark-
to-market losses on fixed-income securities, while higher 
spreads supported profitability.  
Jenny Grimberg: What about residential and commercial 
real estate, where stresses were already visible? 
Tobias Adrian: Real estate markets are faring differently across 
countries. London’s housing market, for example, has cooled 
meaningfully, while other major financial centers like New York 
and Hong Kong continue to see strong home price appreciation. 
Real estate valuations are sensitive to long-term yields, and the 
pace of price appreciation will probably slow if yields remain at 
elevated levels. But with economic activity remaining relatively 
strong, immediate severe distress in either commercial or 
residential real estate seems unlikely.   
Jenny Grimberg: When would you begin to worry that 
higher yields are creating a financial stability problem?  
Tobias Adrian: The speed and magnitude of the yield move 
matter enormously. So far, even though bond yields have risen 
sharply, market functioning has remained orderly and no forced 
deleveraging appears to be occurring, as we discussed. I would 
be more concerned if the move in yields accelerated or became 
nonlinear, which could set off a damaging deleveraging spiral in 
which investors are forced to reduce leverage, adding to selling 
pressures and intensifying market stress. The question then 
becomes: who will catch the falling knife?  
And leveraged investors may not be the only source of selling 
pressures. Before central banks launched massive asset 
purchase programs to stabilize markets in March 2020, open-
ended funds were the sellers, while some hedge funds actually 
played a stabilizing role. Sharp selling pressure can even come 
from plain-vanilla mutual funds or ETFs. That said, it is 
reassuring that the major banks are well capitalized today, 
because it would be particularly worrisome if stress in the non-
bank sector coincided with forced asset sales in the banking 
sector. We saw that dynamic during the GFC and the Long-
Term Capital Management crisis.  
Jenny Grimberg: What should investors watch for warning 
signs of systemic stress? 
Tobias Adrian: I would watch repo market and money market 
functioning, because the early warning signs of stress typically 
show up in short-term funding markets. I would also look at a 
range of market-based indicators, including cross-currency and 
domestic basis measures, swap spreads, and convenience 
yields. I would also watch for a broader tightening of financial 
conditions, which can indicate that pressure is spreading 
beyond a particular market. And I would keep a close eye on 
implied volatility as a market-based assessment of risk that 
feeds directly into investors’ risk-management models. When 
implied volatility rises sharply, investors may need to cut 
positions, which can reinforce the underlying market move.   
Jenny Grimberg: If vulnerabilities were to become more 
acute, how well prepared are policymakers to respond? 
Tobias Adrian: Central banks retain a powerful ability to inject 
liquidity through repo operations, outright asset purchases, and 
emergency standing facilities, and in recent years they have 
repeatedly demonstrated that they can act quickly and 
decisively when market functioning deteriorates. That is distinct 
from the broader stance of monetary policy. Ongoing 
inflationary pressures mean that monetary policy remains on a 
tightening bias in many countries. But central banks can hike 
rates and inject liquidity at the same time.  
Fiscal policymakers are more constrained. Governments may 
still be willing to respond as forcefully as they did during the 
pandemic, but after taking such aggressive fiscal steps, less 
room exists to go big on fiscal policy today. Markets may also 
react to such support more negatively than in 2020 given 
already-elevated debt levels and ongoing concerns about fiscal 
sustainability. If monetary and fiscal authorities act together, as 
they did in 2020, that would temporarily mitigate market stress, 
but it would not resolve the longer-term fiscal problem.  
Hopefully, the recent rise in bond yields will feed back into the 
politics of fiscal policy and prompt an adjustment. Bond market 
pressure has already triggered policy shifts in some countries. 
The question is how far yields must rise before adjustment 
occurs more broadly, and whether that adjustment can happen 
without uncomfortable volatility. But until it occurs, fiscal 
policymakers will remain relatively constrained.     
Jenny Grimberg: Where does all of that leave you on the 
outlook for the global economy and markets? 
Tobias Adrian: It is important to distinguish between the 
baseline and the downside. My baseline is fairly positive. The 
global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in 
the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be 
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, provided they 
do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same 
time, I do worry about downside risks. Beyond the 
vulnerabilities we have discussed, cyber risk is an important 
concern. So far, cyber risks have not been macro-critical, but 
they certainly could become so. AI could strengthen cyber 
defenses, but it could also enhance offensive capabilities. And 
while sophisticated institutions may be resilient to cyberattacks, 
many firms may lack the capabilities and know-how to defend 
themselves effectively. So, plenty of room exists to worry 
about the downside, but the offset is policymaking, especially 
monetary policymaking, as we have discussed. All of that 
leaves me vigilant but cautiously optimistic.
